Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.

</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:

  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works

* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.

I've uploaded a python code repository in the directory missionpinball__mpf__0.33. Consider the following issue description:

<issue_description>
Implement digital_outputs
Most platforms drive lights and coils separately because they need slightly different commands. However, lights may be on coil outputs (mostly for incandescent bulbs) and we support that case by mapping lights to a coil output (via the virtual coils platform). However, we got some more special cases:

- Outputs to drive motors can be either on coil outputs (most custom games) or on lights outputs (Ghostbusters on Spike)
- Flipper enable outputs (System 1/80; Lisy platform)
- GI enable outputs (System 1/80; Lisy)
- AC relay (System 11; Snux platform)

Implement a digital_output which can either drive a light or a driver/coil channel on a platform. Maybe some platforms even got additional logic outputs (such as Aux on the P-Roc).
</issue_description>

## Phase 1. READING: Understanding the Problem<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me break down the issue description:

1. Current situation: Most platforms drive lights and coils separately because they need different commands
2. Existing workaround: Lights can be mapped to coil outputs via virtual coils platform for incandescent bulbs
3. New requirement: Need to support special cases that require digital outputs that can drive either lights OR coils:
   - Motor outputs (can be on coil or light outputs depending on platform)
   - Flipper enable outputs (System 1/80; Lisy platform)
   - GI enable outputs (System 1/80; Lisy)
   - AC relay (System 11; Snux platform)
   - Additional logic outputs (like Aux on P-Roc)

The goal is to implement a "digital_output" that can drive either a light or driver/coil channel on a platform.

Key technical details:
- Need to abstract the concept of digital outputs
- Should work with both light and coil platforms
- Different platforms may have different types of digital outputs
- This is about creating a unified interface for digital outputs regardless of underlying hardware

Steps to reproduce would be:
1. Try to configure a digital output that drives a motor, flipper enable, GI enable, or AC relay
2. Currently this wouldn't work because there's no digital_output abstraction
3. Would need to map these to either lights or coils depending on platform

Best practices:
- Minimal changes to existing code

     1	Mission Pinball Framework (mpf)
     2	===============================
     3	
     4	<img align="right" height="146" src="mpf-logo-200.png"/>
     5	
     6	Let's build a pinball machine!
     7	
     8	The Mission Pinball Framework (MPF) is an open source, cross-platform, software framework for powering real pinball
     9	machines.
    10	
    11	MPF is written in Python 3. It can run on Windows, Mac, and Linux using the same code and configurations.
    12	
    13	MPF interacts with real, physical pinball machines via modern pinball controller hardware such as the Multimorphic
    14	P-ROC or P3-ROC, FAST Pinball controllers, Open Pinball Project (OPP) open source hardware, or Stern SPIKE pinball
    15	machines. You can use it to power your own custom-built machine or to update the software in existing Williams, Bally,
    16	Stern, or Data East machines.
    17	
    18	There's also an [MPF Media Controller](https://github.com/missionpinball/mpf-mc/) (based on [Kivy](http://kivy.org))
    19	which is used to control graphics and sounds, including high-res LCD displays, classic DMDs, and modern RGB LED DMDs.
    20	
    21	MPF is a work-in-progress that's not yet complete, though we're actively developing it and checking in several commits a
    22	week. It's MIT licensed, actively developed by fun people, and supported by a vibrant pinball-loving community.
    23	
    24	The MPF project homepage is here : http://missionpinball.org
    25	
    26	[![Coverage Status](https://coveralls.io/repos/missionpinball/mpf/badge.svg?branch=dev&service=github)](https://coveralls.io/github/missionpinball/mpf?branch=dev)

    48	line in the forum, or make a change and submit a pull request! More info about [Contributing Code](http://docs.missionpinball.org/en/latest/about/contributing_to_mpf.html)

     1	Running Tests
     2	=============
     3	
     4	All tests require the Python "mock" package which you can install via:
     5	
     6	`python -m pip install mock`
     7	
     8	The exact commands you use to run the tests vary depending on whether you have installed MPF to your system or whether
     9	you are running tests from an mpf development folder.
    10	
    11	If you installed MPF
    12	--------------------
    13	If you installed MPF to your system (for example, "pip install mpf"), then you can run the tests from the installed mpf
    14	package via the following command:
    15	
    16	`python -m unittest discover mpf`
    17	
    18	This tells the unittest module to "discover" the tests in the installed mpf module.
    19	
    20	You can also run single tests via:
    21	
    22	`python -m unittest mpf.tests.test_YamlInterface`
    23	
    24	If you want to run tests from an mpf development folder
    25	-------------------------------------------------------
    26	If you're actively developing MPF, then you probably installed MPF in "editable" mode (via pip install -e) which means
    27	the system installation of MPF just contains pointers back to your development folder rather than copying files to
    28	Python's site-packages folder.
    29	
    30	In this case, you need to run tests from your development folder. To do this, you must run the tests from the root
    31	repository folder. (e.g. the parent folder called "mpf" which itself has a child folder called "mpf" in it.) Then from
    32	there, run:
    33	
    34	`python -m unittest discover -s mpf/tests`
    35	
    36	If you want to run a single test, you can run it via:
    37	
    38	`python -m unittest mpf.tests.test_YamlInterface`
    39	
    40	Even with the single test, it's important that you run it from the root mpf folder (which the tests in the child

     1	"""Contains the base class for the Snux driver overlay.
     2	
     3	This class overlays an existing WPC-compatible platform interface to work with
     4	Mark Sunnucks's System 11 interface board.
     5	"""
     6	import asyncio
     7	import logging
     8	
     9	from typing import Any, Optional, Set, Tuple
    10	
    11	from mpf.core.machine import MachineController
    12	from mpf.core.platform import DriverPlatform, DriverConfig
    13	
    14	from mpf.platforms.interfaces.driver_platform_interface import DriverPlatformInterface, PulseSettings, HoldSettings
    15	
    16	from mpf.core.delays import DelayManager
    17	
    18	
    19	# pylint: disable-msg=too-many-instance-attributes
    20	class SnuxHardwarePlatform(DriverPlatform):
    21	
    22	    """Overlay platform for the snux hardware board."""
    23	
    24	    def __init__(self, machine: MachineController) -> None:
    25	        """Initalise the board."""
    26	        super().__init__(machine)
    27	
    28	        self.log = logging.getLogger('Platform.Snux')
    29	        self.delay = DelayManager(machine.delayRegistry)
    30	
    31	        self.platform = None            # type: DriverPlatform
    32	
    33	        self.system11_config = None     # type: Any
    34	        self.snux_config = None         # type: Any
    35	
    36	        self.a_side_queue = set()       # type: Set[Tuple[DriverPlatformInterface, PulseSettings, HoldSettings]]
    37	        self.c_side_queue = set()       # type: Set[Tuple[DriverPlatformInterface, PulseSettings, HoldSettings]]
    38	
    39	        self.a_drivers = set()          # type: Set[DriverPlatformInterface]
    40	        self.c_drivers = set()          # type: Set[DriverPlatformInterface]
    41	
    42	        self.a_side_done_time = 0
    43	        self.c_side_done_time = 0
    44	        self.drivers_holding_a_side = set()     # type: Set[DriverPlatformInterface]
    45	        self.drivers_holding_c_side = set()     # type: Set[DriverPlatformInterface]
    46	        self.a_side_enabled = True
    47	        self.c_side_enabled = False
    48	
    49	        self.ac_relay_in_transition = False
    50	
    51	    def stop(self):
    52	        """Stop the overlay. Nothing to do here because stop is also called on parent platform."""
    53	        pass
    54	
    55	    @property
    56	    def a_side_busy(self):
    57	        """Return if A side cannot be switches off right away."""
    58	        return self.drivers_holding_a_side or self.a_side_done_time > self.machine.clock.get_time() or self.a_side_queue
    59	
    60	    @property
    61	    def c_side_active(self):
    62	        """Return if C side cannot be switches off right away."""
    63	        return self.drivers_holding_c_side or self.c_side_done_time > self.machine.clock.get_time()
    64	
    65	    def _null_log_handler(self, *args, **kwargs):
    66	        pass
    67	
    68	    @asyncio.coroutine
    69	    def initialize(self):
    70	        """Automatically called by the Platform class after all the core modules are loaded."""
    71	        # load coil platform
    72	        self.platform = self.machine.get_platform_sections(
    73	            "platform", getattr(self.machine.config['snux'], 'platform', None))
    74	
    75	        # we have to wait for coils to be initialized
    76	        self.machine.events.add_handler("init_phase_1", self._initialize)
    77	
    78	    def _initialize(self, **kwargs):
    79	        del kwargs
    80	        self._validate_config()
    81	
    82	        self.log.debug("Configuring Snux Diag LED for driver %s",
    83	                       self.snux_config['diag_led_driver'].name)
    84	
    85	        # Hack to silence logging of P_ROC
    86	        # TODO: clean this up
    87	        self.snux_config['diag_led_driver'].hw_driver.log.info = self._null_log_handler
    88	        self.snux_config['diag_led_driver'].hw_driver.log.debug = self._null_log_handler
    89	
    90	        self.snux_config['diag_led_driver'].enable()
    91	
    92	        self.log.debug("Configuring A/C Select Relay for driver %s",
    93	                       self.system11_config['ac_relay_driver'].name)
    94	
    95	        self.system11_config['ac_relay_driver'].get_and_verify_hold_power(1.0)
    96	
    97	        self.log.debug("Configuring A/C Select Relay transition delay for "
    98	                       "%sms", self.system11_config['ac_relay_delay_ms'])
    99	
   100	        self.log.debug("Configuring Flipper Enable for driver %s",
   101	                       self.snux_config['flipper_enable_driver'].name)
   102	
   103	        self.snux_config['flipper_enable_driver'].get_and_verify_hold_power(1.0)
   104	
   105	        self.machine.events.add_handler('init_phase_5',
   106	                                        self._initialize_phase_2)
   107	
   108	    def _initialize_phase_2(self, **kwargs):
   109	        del kwargs
   110	        self.machine.clock.schedule_interval(self._flash_diag_led, 0.5)
   111	
   112	    def _validate_config(self):
   113	        self.system11_config = self.machine.config_validator.validate_config(
   114	            'system11', self.machine.config['system11'])
   115	
   116	        self.snux_config = self.machine.config_validator.validate_config(
   117	            'snux', self.machine.config['snux'])
   118	
   119	    def tick(self):
   120	        """Snux main loop.
   121	
   122	        Called based on the timer_tick event
   123	        """
   124	        if self.a_side_queue:
   125	            self._service_a_side()
   126	        elif self.c_side_queue:
   127	            self._service_c_side()
   128	        elif self.c_side_enabled and not self.c_side_active:
   129	            self._enable_a_side()
   130	
   131	    def _flash_diag_led(self):
   132	        """Flash diagnosis LED."""
   133	        self.snux_config['diag_led_driver'].pulse(250)
   134	
   135	    def configure_driver(self, config: DriverConfig, number: str, platform_settings: dict):
   136	        """Configure a driver on the snux board.
   137	
   138	        Args:
   139	            config: Driver config dict
   140	        """
   141	        orig_number = number
   142	
   143	        if number and (number.endswith('a') or number.lower().endswith('c')):
   144	
   145	            number = number[:-1]
   146	
   147	            platform_driver = self.platform.configure_driver(config, number, platform_settings)
   148	
   149	            snux_driver = SnuxDriver(orig_number, platform_driver, self)
   150	
   151	            if orig_number.lower().endswith('a'):
   152	                self._add_a_driver(snux_driver.platform_driver)
   153	            elif orig_number.lower().endswith('c'):
   154	                self._add_c_driver(snux_driver.platform_driver)
   155	
   156	            return snux_driver
   157	
   158	        else:
   159	            return self.platform.configure_driver(config, number, platform_settings)
   160	
   161	    def set_pulse_on_hit_and_release_rule(self, enable_switch, coil):
   162	        """Configure a rule for a driver on the snux board.
   163	
   164	        Will pass the call onto the parent platform if the driver is not on A/C relay.
   165	        """
   166	        if coil.hw_driver in self.a_drivers or coil.hw_driver in self.c_drivers:
   167	            self.log.warning("Received a request to set a hardware rule for a"
   168	                             "switched driver. Ignoring")
   169	        else:
   170	            self.platform.set_pulse_on_hit_and_release_rule(enable_switch, coil)
   171	
   172	    def set_pulse_on_hit_and_enable_and_release_rule(self, enable_switch, coil):
   173	        """Configure a rule for a driver on the snux board.
   174	
   175	        Will pass the call onto the parent platform if the driver is not on A/C relay.
   176	        """
   177	        if coil.hw_driver in self.a_drivers or coil.hw_driver in self.c_drivers:
   178	            self.log.warning("Received a request to set a hardware rule for a"
   179	                             "switched driver. Ignoring")
   180	        else:
   181	            self.platform.set_pulse_on_hit_and_enable_and_release_rule(enable_switch, coil)
   182	
   183	    def set_pulse_on_hit_and_enable_and_release_and_disable_rule(self, enable_switch, disable_switch, coil):
   184	        """Configure a rule for a driver on the snux board.
   185	
   186	        Will pass the call onto the parent platform if the driver is not on A/C relay.
   187	        """
   188	        if coil.hw_driver in self.a_drivers or coil.hw_driver in self.c_drivers:
   189	            self.log.warning("Received a request to set a hardware rule for a"
   190	                             "switched driver. Ignoring")
   191	        else:
   192	            self.platform.set_pulse_on_hit_and_enable_and_release_and_disable_rule(enable_switch, disable_switch, coil)
   193	
   194	    def set_pulse_on_hit_rule(self, enable_switch, coil):
   195	        """Configure a rule on the snux board.
   196	
   197	        Will pass the call onto the parent platform if the driver is not on A/C relay.
   198	        """
   199	        if coil.hw_driver in self.a_drivers or coil.hw_driver in self.c_drivers:
   200	            self.log.warning("Received a request to set a hardware rule for a"
   201	                             "switched driver. Ignoring")
   202	        else:
   203	            self.platform.set_pulse_on_hit_rule(enable_switch, coil)
   204	
   205	    def clear_hw_rule(self, switch, coil):
   206	        """Clear a rule for a driver on the snux board."""
   207	        self.platform.clear_hw_rule(switch, coil)
   208	
   209	    def driver_action(self, driver, pulse_settings: Optional[PulseSettings], hold_settings: Optional[HoldSettings]):
   210	        """Add a driver action for a switched driver to the queue (for either the A-side or C-side queue).
   211	
   212	        Args:
   213	            driver: A reference to the original platform class Driver instance.
   214	            pulse_settings: Settings for the pulse or None
   215	            hold_settings:Settings for hold or None
   216	
   217	        This action will be serviced immediately if it can, or ASAP otherwise.
   218	        """
   219	        if driver in self.a_drivers:
   220	            self.a_side_queue.add((driver, pulse_settings, hold_settings))
   221	            self._service_a_side()
   222	        elif driver in self.c_drivers:
   223	            self.c_side_queue.add((driver, pulse_settings, hold_settings))
   224	            if not self.ac_relay_in_transition and not self.a_side_busy:
   225	                self._service_c_side()
   226	
   227	    def _enable_ac_relay(self):
   228	        self.system11_config['ac_relay_driver'].enable()
   229	        self.ac_relay_in_transition = True
   230	        self.a_side_enabled = False
   231	        self.c_side_enabled = False
   232	        self.delay.add(ms=self.system11_config['ac_relay_delay_ms'],
   233	                       callback=self._c_side_enabled,
   234	                       name='enable_ac_relay')
   235	
   236	    def _disable_ac_relay(self):
   237	        self.system11_config['ac_relay_driver'].disable()
   238	        self.ac_relay_in_transition = True
   239	        self.a_side_enabled = False
   240	        self.c_side_enabled = False
   241	        self.delay.add(ms=self.system11_config['ac_relay_delay_ms'],
   242	                       callback=self._a_side_enabled,
   243	                       name='disable_ac_relay')
   244	
   245	    # -------------------------------- A SIDE ---------------------------------
   246	
   247	    def _enable_a_side(self):
   248	        if not self.a_side_enabled and not self.ac_relay_in_transition:
   249	
   250	            if self.c_side_active:
   251	                self._disable_all_c_side_drivers()
   252	                self._disable_ac_relay()
   253	                self.delay.add(ms=self.system11_config['ac_relay_delay_ms'],
   254	                               callback=self._enable_a_side,
   255	                               name='enable_a_side')
   256	                return
   257	
   258	            elif self.c_side_enabled:
   259	                self._disable_ac_relay()
   260	
   261	            else:
   262	                self._a_side_enabled()
   263	
   264	    def _a_side_enabled(self):
   265	        self.ac_relay_in_transition = False
   266	        self.a_side_enabled = True
   267	        self.c_side_enabled = False
   268	        self._service_a_side()
   269	
   270	    def _service_a_side(self):
   271	        if not self.a_side_queue:
   272	            return
   273	
   274	        elif not self.a_side_enabled:
   275	            self._enable_a_side()
   276	            return
   277	
   278	        while self.a_side_queue:
   279	            driver, pulse_settings, hold_settings = self.a_side_queue.pop()
   280	
   281	            if hold_settings is None and pulse_settings:
   282	                driver.pulse(pulse_settings)
   283	                self.a_side_done_time = max(self.a_side_done_time,
   284	                                            self.machine.clock.get_time() + (pulse_settings.duration / 1000.0))
   285	
   286	            elif hold_settings and pulse_settings:
   287	                driver.enable(pulse_settings, hold_settings)
   288	                self.drivers_holding_a_side.add(driver)
   289	
   290	            else:  # ms == 0
   291	                driver.disable()
   292	                try:
   293	                    self.drivers_holding_a_side.remove(driver)
   294	                except KeyError:
   295	                    pass
   296	
   297	    def _add_a_driver(self, driver):
   298	        self.a_drivers.add(driver)
   299	
   300	    # -------------------------------- C SIDE ---------------------------------
   301	
   302	    def _enable_c_side(self):
   303	        if (not self.ac_relay_in_transition and
   304	                not self.c_side_enabled and
   305	                not self.a_side_busy):
   306	            self._enable_ac_relay()
   307	
   308	        elif self.c_side_enabled and self.c_side_queue:
   309	            self._service_c_side()
   310	
   311	    def _c_side_enabled(self):
   312	        self.ac_relay_in_transition = False
   313	
   314	        if self.a_side_queue:
   315	            self._enable_a_side()
   316	            return
   317	
   318	        self.a_side_enabled = False
   319	        self.c_side_enabled = True
   320	        self._service_c_side()
   321	
   322	    def _service_c_side(self):
   323	        if not self.c_side_queue:
   324	            return
   325	
   326	        if self.ac_relay_in_transition or self.a_side_busy:
   327	            return
   328	
   329	        elif not self.c_side_enabled:
   330	            self._enable_c_side()
   331	            return
   332	
   333	        while self.c_side_queue:
   334	            driver, pulse_settings, hold_settings = self.c_side_queue.pop()
   335	
   336	            if hold_settings is None and pulse_settings:
   337	                driver.pulse(pulse_settings)
   338	                self.c_side_done_time = max(self.c_side_done_time,
   339	                                            self.machine.clock.get_time() + (pulse_settings.duration / 1000.0))
   340	            elif hold_settings and pulse_settings:
   341	                driver.enable(pulse_settings, hold_settings)
   342	                self.drivers_holding_c_side.add(driver)
   343	
   344	            else:
   345	                driver.disable()
   346	                try:
   347	                    self.drivers_holding_c_side.remove(driver)
   348	                except KeyError:
   349	                    pass
   350	
   351	    def _add_c_driver(self, driver):
   352	        self.c_drivers.add(driver)
   353	
   354	    def _disable_all_c_side_drivers(self):
   355	        if self.c_side_active:
   356	            for driver in self.drivers_holding_c_side:
   357	                driver.disable()
   358	            self.drivers_holding_c_side = set()
   359	            self.c_side_done_time = 0
   360	            self.c_side_enabled = False
   361	
   362	    def validate_coil_section(self, driver, config):
   363	        """Validate coil config for platform."""
   364	        return self.platform.validate_coil_section(driver, config)
   365	
   366	
   367	class SnuxDriver(DriverPlatformInterface):
   368	
   369	    """Represent one driver on the snux board.
   370	
   371	    Two of those drivers may be created for one real driver. One for the A and one for the C side.
   372	    """
   373	
   374	    def __init__(self, number, platform_driver: DriverPlatformInterface, overlay) -> None:
   375	        """Initialize driver."""
   376	        super().__init__(platform_driver.config, number)
   377	        self.number = number
   378	        self.platform_driver = platform_driver
   379	        self.overlay = overlay
   380	
   381	    def __repr__(self):
   382	        """Pretty print."""
   383	        return "SnuxDriver.{}".format(self.number)
   384	
   385	    def get_board_name(self):
   386	        """Return name of driver board."""
   387	        return self.platform_driver.get_board_name()
   388	
   389	    def pulse(self, pulse_settings: PulseSettings):
   390	        """Pulse driver."""
   391	        self.overlay.driver_action(self.platform_driver, pulse_settings, None)
   392	
   393	        # Usually pulse() returns the value (in ms) that the driver will pulse
   394	        # for so we can update Driver.time_when_done. But with A/C switched
   395	        # coils, we don't know when exactly that will be, so we return -1
   396	        return -1
   397	
   398	    def enable(self, pulse_settings: PulseSettings, hold_settings: HoldSettings):
   399	        """Enable driver."""
   400	        self.overlay.driver_action(self.platform_driver, pulse_settings, hold_settings)
   401	
   402	    def disable(self):
   403	        """Disable driver."""
   404	        self.overlay.driver_action(self.platform_driver, None, None)
   405

    28	
    29	    def __init__(self, machine):
    30	        """Initialise virtual platform."""
    31	        super().__init__(machine)
    32	        self._setup_log()
    33	
    34	        # Since the virtual platform doesn't have real hardware, we need to
    35	        # maintain an internal list of switches that were confirmed so we have
    36	        # something to send when MPF needs to know the hardware states of
    37	        # switches
    38	        self.hw_switches = dict()
    39	        self.initial_states_sent = False
    40	        self.features['tickless'] = True
    41	        self._next_driver = 1000
    42	        self._next_switch = 1000
    43	        self._next_light = 1000
    44

    46	        """Return string representation."""
    47	        return '<Platform.Virtual>'
    48

    50	        self.log = logging.getLogger("Virtual Platform")
    51	        self.log.debug("Configuring virtual hardware interface.")
    52	
    53	    @asyncio.coroutine
    54	    def initialize(self):
    55	        """Initialise platform."""
    56	        pass
    57	
    58	    def stop(self):
    59	        """Stop platform."""
    60	        pass

    63	        """Configure a servo device in paltform."""
    64	        return VirtualServo(number)
    65	
    66	    def configure_stepper(self, number: str, config: dict):
    67	        """Configure a smart stepper / axis device in platform."""
    68	        del config
    69	        return VirtualStepper(number)
    70	
    71	    def configure_driver(self, config: DriverConfig, number: str, platform_settings: dict):
    72	        """Configure driver."""
    73	        del platform_settings
    74	        # generate number if None
    75	        if number is None:
    76	            number = self._next_driver
    77	            self._next_driver += 1
    78	
    79	        driver = VirtualDriver(config, number)
    80	
    81	        return driver
    82	
    83	    def configure_switch(self, number: str, config: SwitchConfig, platform_config: dict):
    84	        """Configure switch."""
    85	        # switch needs a number to be distingishable from other switches
    86	        if number is None:
    87	            number = self._next_switch
    88	            self._next_switch += 1
    89	
    90	        # We want to have the virtual platform set all the initial switch states
    91	        # to inactive, so we have to check the config.
    92	        self.hw_switches[number] = config.invert
    93	
    94	        return VirtualSwitch(config, number)
    95

    98	        """Return hw switch states."""

   125	    def _map_channels_to_colors(self, channel_list) -> dict:
   126	        if self.config['type']:
   127	            color_channels = self.config['type']
   128	        else:
   129	            if len(channel_list) == 1:
   130	                # for one channel default to a white channel
   131	                color_channels = "w"
   132	            elif len(channel_list) == 3:
   133	                # for three channels default to RGB
   134	                color_channels = "rgb"
   135	            else:
   136	                raise AssertionError("Please provide a type for light {}. No default for channels {}.".
   137	                                     format(self.name, channel_list))
   138	
   139	        if len(channel_list) != len(color_channels):
   140	            raise AssertionError("Type {} does not match channels {} for light {}".format(
   141	                color_channels, channel_list, self.name
   142	            ))
   143	
   144	        channels = {}   # type: Dict[str, List[Any]]
   145	        for color_name in color_channels:
   146	            # red channel
   147	            if color_name == 'r':
   148	                full_color_name = "red"
   149	            # green channel
   150	            elif color_name == 'g':
   151	                full_color_name = "green"
   152	            # blue channel
   153	            elif color_name == 'b':
   154	                full_color_name = "blue"
   155	            # simple white channel
   156	            elif color_name == 'w':
   157	                full_color_name = "white"
   158	            else:
   159	                raise AssertionError("Invalid element {} in type {} of light {}".format(
   160	                    color_name, self.config['type'], self.name))
   161	
   162	            if full_color_name not in channels:
   163	                channels[full_color_name] = []
   164	            channels[full_color_name].append(channel_list.pop(0))
   165	
   166	        return channels
   167	
   168	    def _load_hw_drivers(self):
   169	        """Load hw drivers."""
   170	        if self.config['platform'] == "drivers":
   171	            channel_list = [
   172	                {
   173	                    "number": self.config['number'],
   174	                    "platform": "drivers"
   175	                }
   176	            ]
   177	            # map channel to color
   178	            channels = self._map_channels_to_colors(channel_list)
   179	        elif not self.config['channels']:
   180	            # get channels from number + platform
   181	            platform = self.machine.get_platform_sections('lights', self.config['platform'])
   182	            try:
   183	                channel_list = platform.parse_light_number_to_channels(self.config['number'], self.config['subtype'])
   184	            except AssertionError as e:
   185	                raise AssertionError("Failed to parse light number {} in platform. See error above".
   186	                                     format(self.name)) from e
   187	
   188	            # copy platform and platform_settings to all channels
   189	            for channel, _ in enumerate(channel_list):
   190	                channel_list[channel]['subtype'] = self.config['subtype']
   191	                channel_list[channel]['platform'] = self.config['platform']
   192	                channel_list[channel]['platform_settings'] = self.config['platform_settings']
   193	            # map channels to colors
   194	            channels = self._map_channels_to_colors(channel_list)
   195	        else:
   196	            if self.config['number'] or self.config['platform'] or self.config['platform_settings']:
   197	                raise AssertionError("Light {} cannot contain platform/platform_settings/number and channels".
   198	                                     format(self.name))
   199	            # alternatively use channels from config
   200	            channels = self.config['channels']

     1	"""Contains the base class for flippers."""
     2	from typing import List
     3	from typing import Optional
     4	
     5	from mpf.core.events import event_handler
     6	from mpf.core.platform_controller import HardwareRule
     7	
     8	from mpf.core.device_monitor import DeviceMonitor
     9	from mpf.core.platform_controller import SwitchRuleSettings, DriverRuleSettings, PulseRuleSettings, HoldRuleSettings
    10	
    11	from mpf.core.system_wide_device import SystemWideDevice
    12	
    13	
    14	@DeviceMonitor(_enabled="enabled")
    15	class Flipper(SystemWideDevice):
    16	
    17	    """Represents a flipper in a pinball machine. Subclass of Device.
    18	
    19	    Contains several methods for actions that can be performed on this flipper,
    20	    like :meth:`enable`, :meth:`disable`, etc.
    21	
    22	    Flippers have several options, including player buttons, EOS swtiches,
    23	    multiple coil options (pulsing, hold coils, etc.)
    24	
    25	    Args:
    26	        machine: A reference to the machine controller instance.
    27	        name: A string of the name you'll refer to this flipper object as.
    28	    """
    29	
    30	    config_section = 'flippers'
    31	    collection = 'flippers'
    32	    class_label = 'flipper'
    33	
    34	    def __init__(self, machine, name):
    35	        """Initialise flipper."""
    36	        super().__init__(machine, name)
    37	
    38	        self._enabled = False
    39	        self._active_rules = []     # type: List[HardwareRule]
    40	
    41	    def _initialize(self):
    42	        if self.config['include_in_ball_search']:
    43	            self.config['playfield'].ball_search.register(
    44	                self.config['ball_search_order'], self._ball_search, self.name)
    45	
    46	    @event_handler(10)
    47	    def enable(self, **kwargs):
    48	        """Enable the flipper by writing the necessary hardware rules to the hardware controller.
    49	
    50	        The hardware rules for coils can be kind of complex given all the
    51	        options, so we've mapped all the options out here. We literally have
    52	        methods to enable the various rules based on the rule letters here,
    53	        which we've implemented below. Keeps it easy to understand. :)
    54	
    55	        Note there's a platform feature saved at:
    56	        self.machine.config['platform']['hw_enable_auto_disable']. If True, it
    57	        means that the platform hardware rules will automatically disable a coil
    58	        that has been enabled when the trigger switch is disabled. If False, it
    59	        means the hardware platform needs its own rule to disable the coil when
    60	        the switch is disabled. Methods F and G below check for that feature
    61	        setting and will not be applied to the hardware if it's True.
    62	
    63	        Two coils, using EOS switch to indicate the end of the power stroke:
    64	        Rule  Type     Coil  Switch  Action
    65	        A.    Enable   Main  Button  active
    66	        D.    Enable   Hold  Button  active
    67	        E.    Disable  Main  EOS     active
    68	
    69	        One coil, using EOS switch (not implemented):
    70	        Rule  Type     Coil  Switch  Action
    71	        A.    Enable   Main  Button  active
    72	        H.    PWM      Main  EOS     active
    73	
    74	        Two coils, not using EOS switch:
    75	        Rule  Type     Coil  Switch  Action
    76	        B.    Pulse    Main  Button  active
    77	        D.    Enable   Hold  Button  active
    78	
    79	        One coil, not using EOS switch:
    80	        Rule  Type       Coil  Switch  Action
    81	        C.    Pulse/PWM  Main  button  active
    82	
    83	        Use EOS switch for safety (for platforms that support mutiple switch
    84	        rules). Note that this rule is the letter "i", not a numeral 1.
    85	        I. Enable power if button is active and EOS is not active
    86	        """
    87	        del kwargs
    88	
    89	        # prevent duplicate enable
    90	        if self._enabled:
    91	            return
    92	
    93	        self._enabled = True
    94	
    95	        self.debug_log('Enabling flipper with config: %s', self.config)
    96	
    97	        # Apply the proper hardware rules for our config
    98	
    99	        if self.config['use_eos']:
   100	            self._enable_main_coil_eos_cutoff_rule()

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Perfect! Now I can see that `ac_relay_driver` is defined in the `system11` section.<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I have a good understanding of the current system:

1. The system already supports mapping lights to coils via the "drivers" platform for lights
2. The Snux platform already uses flipper_enable_driver and diag_led_driver as coils
3. The system11 config already uses ac_relay_driver as a coil
4. All these are currently handled by treating them as regular coils/drivers

The issue is asking for a more generic "digital_output" abstraction that can drive either lights or coils/ drivers. Looking at the examples:
- Motor outputs: can be on coil or light outputs depending on platform 
- Flipper enable outputs: currently configured as coils in snux config
- GI enable outputs: presumably also coils in system11-like configs
- AC relay: currently configured as coils in system11 config
- Additional logic outputs: like Aux on P-Roc

The pattern I see is that these are all essentially digital outputs that can be driven high/low, but they're currently being treated as regular coils or lights depending on the platform.

So I need to:
1. Create a new digital_output device type
2. Allow digital_outputs to be configured to drive either lights or coils
3. Create the appropriate abstraction layer that can handle both underlying types

Looking at the code structure:
- Devices are defined in mpf/devices/
- Config specs are in mpf/core/config_spec.py  
- The existing pattern shows that lights can already use "drivers" platform to wrap coils
- Platforms implement interfaces in mpf/platforms/interfaces/

My approach should be:
1. Define digital_output in config_spec.py
2. Create a digital_output device class in mpf/devices/
3. The digital_output should be able to drive either a light or a coil
4. Follow the existing patterns for how devices work</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 4. TEST CREATION: Creating a Reproduction Script

"""
Test script to reproduce the digital_outputs implementation issue.
This script tests the basic functionality that should be implemented.
"""

import sys
import os
sys.path.insert(0, os.path.join(os.path.dirname(__file__), '..'))

from mpf.tests.MpfTestCase import MpfTestCase


class TestDigitalOutput(MpfTestCase):

    def getConfigFile(self):
        return 'config.yaml'

    def getMachinePath(self):
        return 'test_digital_output/config/'

    def get_platform(self):
        return 'virtual'

    def test_digital_outputs_exist(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs can be configured and accessed."""
        # This should fail initially since digital_outputs don't exist yet
        self.assertIn('test_digital_output_coil', self.machine.digital_outputs)
        self.assertIn('test_digital_output_light', self.machine.digital_outputs)
        self.assertIn('test_digital_output_driver', self.machine.digital_outputs)

    def test_digital_output_types(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs can be configured as coil or light types."""
        if hasattr(self.machine, 'digital_outputs'):
            # Test coil type digital output
            coil_digital = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_coil
            self.assertEqual(coil_digital.config['type'], 'coil')
            
            # Test light type digital output  
            light_digital = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_light
            self.assertEqual(light_digital.config['type'], 'light')
            
            # Test driver type digital output (wraps a coil)
            driver_digital = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_driver
            self.assertEqual(driver_digital.config['type'], 'driver')

    def test_digital_output_operations(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs can be enabled/disabled/pulsed."""
        if hasattr(self.machine, 'digital_outputs'):
            digital_output = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_coil
            
            # These methods should exist for digital outputs
            self.assertTrue(hasattr(digital_output, 'enable'))
            self.assertTrue(hasattr(digital_output, 'disable')) 
            self.assertTrue(hasattr(digital_output, 'pulse'))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    import unittest
    unittest.main()</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/missionpinball__mpf__0.33/test_digital_output/test_digital_output.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Let me run this test to confirm it fails as expected:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/missionpinball__mpf__0.33 && python -m test_digital_output.test_digital_output</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>EEE

"""
Test script to reproduce the digital_outputs implementation issue.
This script tests the basic functionality that should be implemented.
"""

from mpf.tests.MpfTestCase import MpfTestCase


class TestDigitalOutput(MpfTestCase):

    def getConfigFile(self):
        return 'config.yaml'

    def getMachinePath(self):
        return 'test_digital_output/config/'

    def get_platform(self):
        return 'virtual'

    def test_digital_outputs_exist(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs can be configured and accessed."""
        # This should fail initially since digital_outputs don't exist yet
        self.assertIn('test_digital_output_coil', self.machine.digital_outputs)
        self.assertIn('test_digital_output_light', self.machine.digital_outputs)
        self.assertIn('test_digital_output_driver', self.machine.digital_outputs)

    def test_digital_output_types(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs can be configured as coil or light types."""
        if hasattr(self.machine, 'digital_outputs'):
            # Test coil type digital output
            coil_digital = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_coil
            self.assertEqual(coil_digital.config['type'], 'coil')
            
            # Test light type digital output  
            light_digital = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_light
            self.assertEqual(light_digital.config['type'], 'light')
            
            # Test driver type digital output (wraps a coil)
            driver_digital = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_driver
            self.assertEqual(driver_digital.config['type'], 'driver')

    def test_digital_output_operations(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs can be enabled/disabled/pulsed."""
        if hasattr(self.machine, 'digital_outputs'):
            digital_output = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_coil
            
            # These methods should exist for digital outputs
            self.assertTrue(hasattr(digital_output, 'enable'))
            self.assertTrue(hasattr(digital_output, 'disable')) 
            self.assertTrue(hasattr(digital_output, 'pulse'))


if __name__ == '__main__':
    import unittest

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Now I have a clear understanding of the problem and how to fix it:

**Problem Statement:**
The system needs to implement a generic "digital_output" abstraction that can drive either lights or coils/drivers. Currently, special cases like flipper enable drivers, GI enable outputs, AC relays, and motor outputs are handled individually by treating them as regular coils or lights, but there's no unified interface.

**Where the problem is located:**
1. Missing "digital_outputs" section in config_spec.py 
2. Missing digital_output device class in mpf/devices/
3. Missing integration with existing light and driver systems

**How the test reproduces the issue:**
The test tries to configure digital_outputs in the config file, but the system throws a KeyError because "digital_outputs" is not defined in the config specification.

**Best practices to take into account:**
1. Follow existing patterns in the codebase (like how lights and drivers work)
2. Maintain backward compatibility
3. Support both coil and light backing types
4. Reuse existing interfaces where possible
5. Keep the implementation minimal and focused

**How to fix the problem:**
1. Add "digital_outputs" section to config_spec.py with appropriate fields (number, type, etc.)
2. Create a digital_output device class in mpf/devices/ that can wrap either a light or a driver
3. The digital_output should support enable/disable/pulse operations like drivers do
4. Follow the same pattern as DriverLight class but make it more generic

Looking at the existing code patterns:
- Drivers have enable/disable/pulse methods
- Lights have color/brightness methods
- The DriverLight class already bridges the gap by wrapping a driver to act like a light
- I need to create the inverse - a class that can wrap either a light or driver to provide digital output functionality

The digital_output should:
- Have a "type" field (coil, light, driver)
- Have a "number" field that references the underlying device
- Support enable(), disable(), pulse() methods
- Internally delegate to the wrapped device</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>## Phase 6. FIX IMPLEMENTATION

from mpf.core.device_monitor import DeviceMonitor
from mpf.core.system_wide_device import SystemWideDevice
from mpf.platforms.interfaces.driver_platform_interface import PulseSettings, HoldSettings


@DeviceMonitor(_enabled="enabled")
class DigitalOutput(SystemWideDevice):

    """A digital output in a pinball machine."""

    config_section = 'digital_outputs'
    collection = 'digital_outputs'
    class_label = 'digital_output'

    def __init__(self, machine, name):
        """Initialise digital output."""
        super().__init__(machine, name)
        self._enabled = False
        self._target_device = None

    def _initialize(self):
        """Initialise the digital output."""
        # Based on the type, link to the appropriate underlying device
        if self.config['type'] == 'coil' or self.config['type'] == 'driver':
            # Link to a coil/driver
            if self.config['number'] in self.machine.coils:
                self._target_device = self.machine.coils[self.config['number']]
            else:
                self.raise_config_error("Referenced coil '{}' not found".format(self.config['number']), 1)
        elif self.config['type'] == 'light':
            # Link to a light
            if self.config['number'] in self.machine.lights:
                self._target_device = self.machine.lights[self.config['number']]
            else:
                self.raise_config_error("Referenced light '{}' not found".format(self.config['number']), 2)
        else:
            self.raise_config_error("Invalid type '{}' for digital_output".format(self.config['type']), 3)

    def enable(self, **kwargs):
        """Enable the digital output."""
        del kwargs
        self._enabled = True
        
        if self.config['type'] == 'coil' or self.config['type'] == 'driver':
            # Enable the underlying driver
            if hasattr(self._target_device, 'enable'):
                # Get default settings from the driver
                pulse_ms = self._target_device.config.get('default_pulse_ms', 30)
                pulse_power = self._target_device.config.get('default_pulse_power', 1.0)
                hold_power = self._target_device.config.get('default_hold_power', 1.0)
                
                pulse_settings = PulseSettings(power=pulse_power, duration=pulse_ms)
                hold_settings = HoldSettings(power=hold_power)
                self._target_device.enable(pulse_settings=pulse_settings, hold_settings=hold_settings)
        elif self.config['type'] == 'light':
            # Turn the light on at full brightness
            self._target_device.color("white")

    def disable(self, **kwargs):
        """Disable the digital output."""
        del kwargs
        self._enabled = False
        
        if self.config['type'] == 'coil' or self.config['type'] == 'driver':
            # Disable the underlying driver
            if hasattr(self._target_device, 'disable'):
                self._target_device.disable()
        elif self.config['type'] == 'light':
            # Turn the light off
            self._target_device.off()

    def pulse(self, milliseconds=None, **kwargs):
        """Pulse the digital output."""
        del kwargs
        if self.config['type'] == 'coil' or self.config['type'] == 'driver':
            # Pulse the underlying driver
            if milliseconds is None:
                milliseconds = self._target_device.config.get('default_pulse_ms', 30)
            
            if hasattr(self._target_device, 'pulse'):
                pulse_settings = PulseSettings(power=1.0, duration=milliseconds)
                return self._target_device.pulse(pulse_settings=pulse_settings)
        elif self.config['type'] == 'light':
            # Flash the light briefly
            if milliseconds is None:
                milliseconds = 30
            self._target_device.color("white")
            # Schedule turning off after pulse duration
            self.machine.clock.schedule_once(lambda dt: self._target_device.off(), milliseconds / 1000.0)</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/missionpinball__mpf__0.33/mpf/devices/digital_output.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now let me test if my implementation works by running the test:<tool_call>execute_bash<arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>cd /workspace/missionpinball__mpf__0.33 && python -m unittest mpf.tests.test_DigitalOutputs -v</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>test_digital_output_operations (mpf.tests.test_DigitalOutputs.TestDigitalOutputs)

from mpf.tests.MpfTestCase import MpfTestCase
from unittest.mock import MagicMock


class TestDigitalOutputs(MpfTestCase):

    def getConfigFile(self):
        return 'config.yaml'

    def getMachinePath(self):
        return 'tests/machine_files/digital_outputs/'

    def get_platform(self):
        return 'virtual'

    def test_digital_outputs_exist(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs can be configured and accessed."""
        # This should pass now that digital_outputs exist
        self.assertIn('test_digital_output_coil', self.machine.digital_outputs)
        self.assertIn('test_digital_output_light', self.machine.digital_outputs)
        self.assertIn('test_digital_output_driver', self.machine.digital_outputs)

    def test_digital_output_types(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs can be configured as coil or light types."""
        # Test coil type digital output
        coil_digital = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_coil
        self.assertEqual(coil_digital.config['type'], 'coil')
        
        # Test light type digital output  
        light_digital = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_light
        self.assertEqual(light_digital.config['type'], 'light')
        
        # Test driver type digital output (wraps a coil)
        driver_digital = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_driver
        self.assertEqual(driver_digital.config['type'], 'driver')

    def test_digital_output_operations(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs can be enabled/disabled/pulsed."""
        digital_output = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_coil
        
        # These methods should exist for digital outputs
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(digital_output, 'enable'))
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(digital_output, 'disable')) 
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(digital_output, 'pulse'))

    def test_digital_output_coil_operations(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs backed by coils work correctly."""
        digital_output = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_coil
        coil = self.machine.coils.coil_01
        
        # Mock the coil methods
        coil.enable = MagicMock()
        coil.disable = MagicMock()
        coil.pulse = MagicMock(return_value=30)
        
        # Test enable
        digital_output.enable()
        self.assertTrue(digital_output._enabled)
        coil.enable.assert_called_once()
        
        # Test disable
        digital_output.disable()
        self.assertFalse(digital_output._enabled)
        coil.disable.assert_called_once()
        
        # Test pulse
        result = digital_output.pulse(50)
        coil.pulse.assert_called_once()
        self.assertIsNotNone(result)

    def test_digital_output_light_operations(self):
        """Test that digital_outputs backed by lights work correctly."""
        digital_output = self.machine.digital_outputs.test_digital_output_light
        light = self.machine.lights.light_01
        
        # Mock the light methods
        light.color = MagicMock()
        light.off = MagicMock()
        
        # Test enable (should turn light on)
        digital_output.enable()
        self.assertTrue(digital_output._enabled)
        light.color.assert_called_once_with("white")
        
        # Test disable (should turn light off)
        digital_output.disable()
        self.assertFalse(digital_output._enabled)
        light.off.assert_called_once()
        
        # Test pulse (should flash light)
        digital_output.pulse(100)
        # Should have called color with white
        self.assertEqual(light.color.call_count, 2)
        light.color.assert_any_call("white")</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>"""Test digital outputs."""

from mpf.tests.MpfTestCase import MpfTestCase


class TestDigitalOutputsExample(MpfTestCase):

    def getConfigFile(self):
        return 'example_usage.yaml'

    def getMachinePath(self):
        return 'tests/machine_files/digital_outputs/'

    def get_platform(self):
        return 'virtual'

    def test_example_digital_outputs(self):
        """Test that all example digital outputs can be configured and accessed."""
        # Test all digital outputs from the example
        self.assertIn('motor_output_coil', self.machine.digital_outputs)
        self.assertIn('motor_output_light', self.machine.digital_outputs)
        self.assertIn('flipper_enable_output', self.machine.digital_outputs)
        self.assertIn('gi_enable_output', self.machine.digital_outputs)
        self.assertIn('ac_relay_output', self.machine.digital_outputs)

        # Test types
        self.assertEqual(self.machine.digital_outputs.motor_output_coil.config['type'], 'coil')
        self.assertEqual(self.machine.digital_outputs.motor_output_light.config['type'], 'light')
        self.assertEqual(self.machine.digital_outputs.flipper_enable_output.config['type'], 'coil')
        self.assertEqual(self.machine.digital_outputs.gi_enable_output.config['type'], 'coil')
        self.assertEqual(self.machine.digital_outputs.ac_relay_output.config['type'], 'coil')

        # Test operations
        motor_coil = self.machine.digital_outputs.motor_output_coil
        motor_light = self.machine.digital_outputs.motor_output_light
        flipper_enable = self.machine.digital_outputs.flipper_enable_output

        # All should have enable/disable/pulse methods
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(motor_coil, 'enable'))
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(motor_coil, 'disable'))
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(motor_coil, 'pulse'))

        self.assertTrue(hasattr(motor_light, 'enable'))
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(motor_light, 'disable'))
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(motor_light, 'pulse'))

        self.assertTrue(hasattr(flipper_enable, 'enable'))
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(flipper_enable, 'disable'))
        self.assertTrue(hasattr(flipper_enable, 'pulse'))</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>File created successfully at: /workspace/missionpinball__mpf__0.33/mpf/tests/test_DigitalOutputsExample.py</tool_response><|assistant|></think>
